## Run 179 — REPAIR — discovery-pool table continuity and parser fail-closed guard — 2026-09-12

**RunId:** `2026-09-12T13:10:28Z-repair-179`

The first publication pass after Run 178 returned `COMPLETE — 374/374` at `2026-09-12T13:09:43.473Z` even though the global utility ledger file visibly contained row 375. The new row had been separated from row 374 by a blank line. `parseLedger` stopped at that blank line, silently ignored the later track row and published only the preceding 374 identities.

This is an actionable objective defect. REPAIR removes the accidental blank line so row 375 is contiguous and extends the shared Spotify ledger parser with a fail-closed check: any later numeric Markdown row containing a Spotify track URI after table termination now throws `Ledger contains a track row after table termination on row …` instead of allowing a false COMPLETE receipt. A regression test reproduces the split-row failure. The focused publisher-ledger suite passes 6/6.

No musical decision changed. Extra Medium — *My Sound* remains PARKED only at `Same Way → candidate → U Belong 2 Me`, placement-specifically rejected only at `Can I live → candidate → Invisible Warriors Of Light`, and retained as genuinely interesting in EDITORIAL ENGINE — DISCOVERY POOL. STRANGE GAIT remains 49 canonical tracks; its protected trio remains contiguous.

**RUN ANALYSIS:** Approximately 44 inspected → 9 ranked leads → 5 exact resolutions + 4 unresolved → 3 selected evaluations + 2 unevaluated alternates → **1 ADD + 2 PARKED** → 3 utility additions. Lead conversion improved from Run 86's 3/34 (8.8%) to 9/44 (20.5%); exact-resolution conversion was 5/9 (55.6%); selected-candidate-to-ADD conversion was 1/3 (33.3%). The consecutive no-ADD count is 0; recent canonical ADD rate is 2/10 across Runs 78–87 and 2/33 across Runs 55–87. The strongest counterargument is that the 130 BPM Wheelman insertion could make the central run feel overextended or require a tempo reset that its paper evidence cannot carry. That remains the principal risk. The run proves the repaired funnel admitted and seriously evaluated more credible contenders without turning UNKNOWN into an automatic veto. It does not prove the Wheelman handoffs, phrase fit, perceived pulse or live-mixing compatibility, does not disqualify Secret Desire globally, does not establish that Poison's 2009 Spotify master is the 2026 remaster, and does not create an ADD quota.

## Run 86 — EXPLORE — stagnation-aware placement search — 2026-09-08

**Pre-audit:** EXPLORE. Run 85 left STRANGE GAIT at 14 canonical tracks and EDITORIAL ENGINE — DISCOVERY POOL at 146 exact tracks with matching COMPLETE receipts. No REVISIT was TRIGGERED, no listener repair was authorized, the generated map matched canonical state, automatic publishing remained enabled, and the two listener-protected handoffs stayed intact. No actionable objective musical defect was present. The 31 consecutive no-ADD runs since Run 54 were treated as a workflow-health signal: discovery remained productive while canonical admission had stalled. This run therefore prioritized tracks with an evidence-backed function inside an existing passage rather than merely conspicuous rhythmic credentials. The ADD bar was not lowered.

### Canonical evaluations

#### Peverelist — Pulse I — ADD

- **Volume fit:** PASS. Resident Advisor describes the exact track as quietly inventive broken techno with crooked formation, percussive clacks / woodblock-style percussion and a gradual introduction of skeletal new elements. This is track-specific rhythmic and structural evidence, not novelty as a substitute for fit.
- **Immediate transition:** PASS provisionally at `Tehutis Law → Pulse I → Fragments`. The measured working path is `139 → 136 → 140 BPM`, with the independent 133-BPM conflict preserved. Close tempo is supporting evidence only. The transition-scale purpose is explicit: after Tehutis Law's provisional re-grounding, Pulse I introduces a crooked accumulation stage before Fragments' provisional driving re-escalation. Entry, exit, phrasing and live-mix feel remain unknown, but the repository does not require direct audio proof for a provisional placement when the best lawful evidence supports a deliberate transition-scale function.
- **Surrounding three-to-five-track run:** PASS provisionally. The local run becomes `Thick to Handle → Tehutis Law → Pulse I → Fragments → Drummz`: provisional deformation → re-grounding → crooked accumulation → re-escalation → restless hinge. Those role labels are editorial interpretation, not measured sonic facts. The addition gives the middle passage a legible developmental step rather than inserting a track merely because it is unusual.
- **Long-form journey:** PASS provisionally. The 6:50 insertion expands the middle without touching either listener-protected handoff and without defaulting growth to the tail. Its documented gradual construction supplies a journey function distinct from simply demonstrating another groove or production world.
- **Verdict:** **ADD** between Jayson Wynters — Tehutis Law and CHANCEKNOT — Fragments. Provisional; audio and live mixing remain untested.

#### Jump Source — A Dull Knife (feat. Harmony Index) — placement-specific REJECT / retained discovery

- **Volume fit:** PASS at the adjacent edge. Resident Advisor describes the exact track beginning as a dub-techno groover and growing into warm syncopated bass music when Harmony Index enters. The internal transformation is genuinely useful discovery evidence.
- **Immediate transition:** FAIL for the two tested placements only. Exact 120 BPM produces measured `Cave 134 → candidate 120 → Too Much Time 140` and `Truth in Noise 138 → candidate 120 → Transparency 143*` paths. BPM is not energy or flow and a hard turn can pass; here, however, the available evidence does not explain why either conspicuous tempo reset improves those exact boundaries.
- **Surrounding three-to-five-track run:** FAIL for the proposed placements. In the early run the 120-BPM insertion would sit inside `136 → 136 → 134 → 120 → 140 → 140`; before the protected trio it would create `Drummz 138 → Truth in Noise 138 → 120 → Transparency 143* → When We Froze 140`. These numbers do not prove bad flow, but without track-specific evidence connecting the reset to either local run, the placement purpose is weaker than the disruption it introduces.
- **Long-form journey:** NOT PROVEN for these placements. The different-world transformation remains interesting, but this run does not establish a net journey gain at either tested boundary.
- **Verdict:** **REJECT the two Run 86 placements only**, not the track globally. Retain locally and in the doctrine-free global utility playlist as genuinely interesting.

`*` Transparency's 143/107 BPM conflict remains preserved; the placement verdict does not rely on treating 143 as perceived pulse.

The unresolved `Suspension of Disbelief` lead receives no ADD/REJECT/REVISIT verdict.

### Auditor approval

**APPROVED.** The immutable schemaVersion 2 request is pinned to the live pre-request SHA and the matching PARTIAL snapshot is internally valid: runId, sourceCommit, mode and target match; 3 requested = 2 selected exact resolutions + 1 unresolved; the exact resolver error and Pulse I warning are preserved; no stale candidate, duplicate, substitute or alternate entered evaluation. Both selected candidates were judged separately at volume, transition, local-run and long-form scales. BPM was used as measured evidence rather than energy or mixability. Pulse I's 136/133 metadata conflict is preserved. The protected `Transparency → When We Froze → Stardancer` internal order remains untouched. Auditor approves the Pulse I ADD, the two-placement A Dull Knife rejection, local inspiration retention, and two global utility additions.

### Durable result

- STRANGE GAIT: **14 → 15 canonical tracks**; add Peverelist — Pulse I after Tehutis Law and before Fragments; no other reorder.
- Protected internal order remains `Transparency → When We Froze → Stardancer`.
- Local inspiration: Pulse I exact canonical ADD; A Dull Knife exact placement-specific REJECT / interesting; Suspension of Disbelief unresolved research lead; current Orbe Records and Repetitive Rhythm Research release windows retained as watches without invented track facts.
- EDITORIAL ENGINE — DISCOVERY POOL: **146 → 148 exact tracks**, adding Pulse I and A Dull Knife.
- Journey-map annotations: add Pulse I as provisional Track 9 / crooked-accumulation hinge and renumber downstream positional labels; generated map outputs are left to GitHub Actions.
- Audio analysis: **NOT TESTED**. Live mixing: **NOT TESTED**.

**RUN ANALYSIS:** Approximately 34 inspected → 3 ranked leads → 2 exact resolutions + 1 unresolved → 2 evaluations → **1 ADD + 1 placement-specific REJECT** → 2 utility additions. The pre-run stagnation count was **31 consecutive runs without a canonical ADD**, last ADD Run 54. Run 86 ends that drought; it is the first ADD in 32 runs counting Runs 55–86. The important workflow finding is not that the bar should be looser: repeated prior evaluations had effectively promoted missing direct handoff audio from an uncertainty into a veto, even though the repository permits provisional best-evidence sequencing. Pulse I clears the bar because its exact evidence supplies a concrete transition-scale purpose, a coherent local-run function and a long-form gain without touching protected state. A Dull Knife demonstrates the opposite: interesting exact discovery value does not override an unsupported placement. The run does not prove Pulse I's live transition or phrase fit, does not globally reject A Dull Knife, and does not prove STRANGE GAIT is complete.
